<a id="oman-migration-guide"></a>

# Oman: migration guide

Sources checked up to 10 Nov 2026.

How to go to Oman from Bangladesh, visa by visa, step by step, for Bangladesh passport holders, as of November 2026. A raised number such as 3 is an official source; N3 is a news, NGO (non-governmental organisation), research or guide source, listed under "Needs an official source"; a year, like N3 (2022), marks a source older than 2025. "Need to know" marks a point no source has yet confirmed. "You'll need:" lists what to have before starting a procedure.

RO is the Omani rial (1 rial is 1,000 baisa) <sup>[N1](#n1)</sup>; Tk is the Bangladeshi taka (1 lakh is Tk 100,000). The rial is pegged at RO 0.3845 to the US dollar, and Bangladesh Bank's rate on 19 October 2026 was Tk 123.65 to the dollar, so RO 1 is about Tk 322 <sup>[1](#o1), [2](#o2)</sup>. Since 31 October 2023 Oman has issued no new visas to Bangladesh passport holders except in the exempt categories set out under Is it open now <sup>[N2 (2023)](#n2), [N3 (2024)](#n3)</sup>.

<a id="background"></a>

## 1 Background

<a id="in-one-minute"></a>

### 1.1 In one minute

Oman has been Bangladesh's third-largest destination since 2004, but since 31 October 2023 it has issued no new ordinary work visas to Bangladeshis: Royal Oman Police (ROP), which issues Oman's visas, suspended all new visas for them "till further notice" <sup>[3](#o3), [N2 (2023)](#n2)</sup>. BMET (the Bureau of Manpower, Employment and Training, the Bangladesh government body that registers and clears migrant workers) cleared 127,822 workers for Oman in 2023, then 357 in 2024, 671 in 2025 and 506 from 1 January to 8 November 2026 <sup>[4](#o4), [5](#o5), [6](#o6), [7](#o7)</sup>. Since June 2024 only family, professional (doctors, engineers, nurses, teachers, accountants), investor, official and some visitor visas are open, and in August 2026 a quota of 5,000 Bangladeshi farm workers was reported approved for members of Oman's farmers' associations <sup>[N3 (2024)](#n3), [N4](#n4)</sup>. Oman's labour minister promised in January 2026 that work visas would reopen within two months; in August 2026 the Bangladesh Embassy in Muscat called posts about Oman's labour market opening or "free visas" rumours, and in September 2026 Bangladesh's state minister for foreign affairs said visas for Bangladeshis remained restricted <sup>[N5](#n5), [N6](#n6), [N7](#n7)</sup>. When Oman was open, a job started as an Omani sponsor's Ministry of Labour authorisation to bring a foreign worker, and such authorisations were sold on as "free visas": workers paid Tk 2 to 3.5 lakh against a legal maximum of Tk 1,00,780 for a man, and some arrived to find no work <sup>[8](#o8), [N8 (2016)](#n8), [N9 (2021)](#n9), [N10 (2023)](#n10), [9 (2019)](#o9)</sup>. About 599,000 Bangladeshis still work in Oman, the largest foreign group, many on wages near RO 90 a month <sup>[10](#o10), [N9 (2021)](#n9)</sup>.

<a id="is-it-open-now-and-who-can-go"></a>

### 1.2 Is it open now, and who can go?

<a id="status"></a>

#### 1.2.1 Status

*Status, 9 November 2026: closed to new ordinary work visas; a few categories and one farm-worker quota are open.* ROP suspended "the issuance of all types of new visas for Bangladeshi Citizens" from Tuesday 31 October 2023, and on the same day stopped all nationalities converting a tourist or visit visa into a work visa inside Oman <sup>[N2 (2023)](#n2)</sup>. In September 2026 Bangladesh's state minister for foreign affairs said visas for Bangladeshis to Oman remain restricted <sup>[N7](#n7), [N11](#n11)</sup>. BMET cleared 506 workers for Oman from 1 January to 8 November 2026, Oman's 33rd place among destinations <sup>[7](#o7)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Has ROP or the Ministry of Labour (MoL) published the suspension, or the June 2024 exemptions, as a formal notice anyone can check?

<a id="what-is-open-and-for-whom"></a>

#### 1.2.2 What is open, and for whom

- **Exempt categories (since June 2024).** Oman's embassy in Dhaka exempted family visas, visit visas for Bangladeshis living in another GCC (Gulf Cooperation Council) country, physicians, engineers, nurses, teachers, accountants, investors, all official visas and high-income tourists <sup>[N3 (2024)](#n3), [N12 (2024)](#n12)</sup>. The embassy takes these applications in Dhaka with attested papers and coordinates with ROP; it said processing takes one to four weeks <sup>[N3 (2024)](#n3)</sup>. Bangladesh's ambassador to Oman said in June 2026 that visas were open in 13 categories <sup>[N13](#n13)</sup>. The steps are under the visas that own them.
- **Still closed.** New visas for general workers in construction, agriculture and services stayed closed through July 2026 <sup>[N13](#n13)</sup>, and in September 2026 airport immigration officers described Oman as suspending general worker visas and the recruitment of women domestic workers <sup>[N14](#n14)</sup>. Bangladeshi business owners in Oman said in July 2026 that the hiring ban had left restaurants, supermarkets, salons, farms and transport short of staff <sup>[N13](#n13)</sup>.
- **Farm workers, 2026 quota.** On 6 August 2026 the Omani Agricultural Association's Al Dhahirah branch opened applications (9 to 20 August, at its office in Al Murtafa'a, Ibri) for registered farmers to recruit Bangladeshi farm workers, under MoL and ROP approvals <sup>[N4](#n4)</sup>. A Sanad (government-services) office owner said 5,000 workers were approved for members of farmers' associations across Oman, up to 330 per governorate or association <sup>[N4](#n4)</sup>. Farmers had to be paid-up association members (RO 25 a year) with a farm-holding certificate and a valid MoL work permit <sup>[N4](#n4)</sup>. The Bangladesh Embassy in Muscat said the notice covered only limited visas for farm work in the Al Dhahirah region and did not open the labour market <sup>[N6](#n6)</sup>. Farm labourers (208) and farm workers (33) are the largest groups in BMET's 2026 clearances for Oman <sup>[11](#o11)</sup>.
- **Farm and fishing workers, 2026 agreement.** On 9 September 2026 the state minister said Oman wants 5,000 farm workers and 5,000 fishermen and an agreement had been signed <sup>[N7](#n7), [N11](#n11)</sup>. A six-member MoL delegation then met Expatriates' Welfare Ministry officials in Dhaka; formalities must be completed before hiring starts, and an official of BOESL (the state-owned Bangladesh Overseas Employment and Services Ltd) said a top member of the Omani government will decide on reopening <sup>[N15](#n15)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Which Bangladeshi agencies hold BMET permission for the 2026 farm-worker visas, and what are workers being charged for them?
> - Will the 5,000 fishermen be recruited through BOESL, private agencies or Omani associations, and when?
> - Can a Bangladeshi already in Oman still change employer or renew his residence card under the suspension?

<a id="the-2023-suspension-why-and-what-has-happened-since"></a>

#### 1.2.3 The 2023 suspension: why, and what has happened since

ROP gave no reason <sup>[N10 (2023)](#n10)</sup>. What each side said:
- **Oman.** Its embassy in Dhaka called it a temporary step to align with the Omani labour market's demand and stability, "purely for technical reasons", and not political <sup>[N16 (2023)](#n16), [N3 (2024)](#n3)</sup>. In January 2026 Oman's labour minister, Dr Mahad bin Saeed Baowain, said the aim was to regularise irregular and undocumented expatriates first <sup>[N5](#n5), [N17](#n17)</sup>.
- **Bangladesh Embassy, Muscat.** There were not enough jobs for the Bangladeshis already there; many who came without a confirmed job were jobless or underpaid, and 50 to 100 people came to the embassy every day asking to go home <sup>[N16 (2023)](#n16)</sup>. Jobless men fell to brokers and were trafficked, many trying to reach Europe through Iran or Turkey <sup>[N16 (2023)](#n16)</sup>.
- **Diplomatic and Omani sources, to Prothom Alo, Nov 2023.** Oman stopped visas to stop their misuse; a source from Oman said Oman had recently let each young Omani get approval to employ five workers, and traffickers used these approvals to send Bangladeshis without checking there was a job <sup>[N18 (2023)](#n18)</sup>. Oman's coast guard had been detaining people, many of them Bangladeshi, carried on small Iran-UAE boats with the promise of Europe <sup>[N18 (2023)](#n18)</sup>.
- **Bangladesh government, Nov 2023.** The foreign minister said traffickers had abused the route <sup>[N18 (2023)](#n18)</sup>.
- **Bangladeshi press, May 2024.** The suspension was meant to stop traffickers exploiting Omani visas, deal with an oversupply of foreign workers and create jobs for Omanis <sup>[N19 (2024)](#n19)</sup>.

The day before the suspension, 30 October 2023, the Bangladesh Embassy in Muscat began requiring workers' last three months' salary sheets before attesting a visa or agreement <sup>[N10 (2023)](#n10)</sup>. An Omani delegation had been in Dhaka on 11 to 14 October 2023 proposing two memoranda of understanding, including on illegal migration and trafficking <sup>[N10 (2023)](#n10)</sup>.

Since then:
- **June 2024:** the exempt categories above opened <sup>[N3 (2024)](#n3), [N12 (2024)](#n12)</sup>.
- **2025:** Oman let foreigners without valid papers regularise or leave without overstay fines until 31 July 2025, extended to 31 December 2025, through ROP offices <sup>[N20](#n20)</sup>.
- **26 January 2026, Riyadh:** the labour minister told adviser Asif Nazrul work visas would reopen within two months; the adviser praised Oman for regularising undocumented Bangladeshis without fines and urged Oman to sign the already-finalised labour MoU <sup>[N5](#n5)</sup>.
- **12 August 2026:** the Bangladesh Embassy in Muscat said Oman had made no announcement on recruiting Bangladeshi workers or opening its labour market <sup>[N6](#n6)</sup>.
- **September to October 2026:** the farm and fishing agreement and the MoL delegation's visit to Dhaka <sup>[N7](#n7), [N15](#n15)</sup>. In late October BOESL's managing director said efforts were under way for Oman to reopen its market to Bangladeshi workers <sup>[N15](#n15)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Was the regularisation the adviser praised in January 2026 a separate scheme for Bangladeshis, or the 2025 grace period, and how many Bangladeshis used it?
> - Has the Bangladesh-Oman labour MoU been signed?

<a id="the-size-of-the-flow"></a>

#### 1.2.4 The size of the flow

From 2004 to May 2025, 1,653,744 workers went to Oman, 11.85% of all Bangladeshi overseas employment, third after Saudi Arabia (34.49%) and the UAE (15.82%) <sup>[3](#o3)</sup>. In 2023 Oman was BMET's third destination, after Saudi Arabia and Malaysia <sup>[4](#o4)</sup>. Workers BMET cleared for Oman, by year:
- 2014: 105,748 <sup>[3](#o3)</sup>
- 2015: 129,859 <sup>[3](#o3)</sup>
- 2016: 188,247 <sup>[3](#o3)</sup>
- 2017: 89,074 <sup>[3](#o3)</sup>
- 2018: 72,504 <sup>[3](#o3)</sup>
- 2019: 72,654 <sup>[3](#o3)</sup>
- 2020: 21,071 <sup>[3](#o3)</sup>
- 2021: 55,009 <sup>[3](#o3)</sup>
- 2022: 179,612 <sup>[3](#o3)</sup>
- 2023: 127,822 <sup>[4](#o4)</sup>
- 2024: 357 <sup>[5](#o5)</sup>
- 2025: 671 <sup>[6](#o6)</sup>
- 2026, to 8 November: 506 <sup>[7](#o7)</sup>

Around the suspension, by month: July 2023 10,362; August 10,541; September 9,570; October 9,669; November 9,435; December 1,920; January 2024 301; February 0; March 0 <sup>[12](#o12), [13](#o13), [14](#o14), [15](#o15), [16](#o16), [17](#o17), [18](#o18), [19](#o19), [20](#o20)</sup>. Of the 671 cleared in 2025, 435 were cleared as "A Builder" <sup>[21](#o21)</sup>.

*How many Bangladeshis are in Oman.* Oman's National Centre for Statistics and Information (NCSI) counted 599,070 Bangladeshi expatriate workers in September 2026, still the largest nationality, ahead of 529,810 Indians and 282,986 Pakistanis, out of 1,854,441 expatriate workers <sup>[10](#o10)</sup>. The count was 703,840 in mid-2023 and 637,152 in June 2024 <sup>[N10 (2023)](#n10), [N21](#n21)</sup>. Remittance from Oman rose from USD 1.66 billion in FY2024-25 to USD 2.05 billion in FY2025-26, Expatriates' Welfare Minister Ariful Haque Choudhury said <sup>[N22](#n22)</sup>.

*Against India, Pakistan and Nepal.* In the year to September 2026 the number of Bangladeshi workers in Oman fell 3.4% and Pakistanis 7.1%, while Indians rose 2.3% <sup>[10](#o10)</sup>; Egyptians rose 16.8% to 58,282 and Myanmar workers 3.0% to 38,518 <sup>[10](#o10)</sup>. Oman has cut intake from Pakistan too: Pakistan registered 91,570 workers for Oman over 2024-2026, but only 9,373 in 2025 and 607 in 2026 <sup>[N23](#n23)</sup>. India issued 33,482 emigration clearances for Oman in 2021-2024 <sup>[22 (2024)](#o22)</sup>. Nepal is a small sender: 1,508 Nepali workers went to Oman through recruitment agencies in 2022/23 <sup>[23](#o23)</sup>.

<a id="who-qualifies"></a>

#### 1.2.5 Who qualifies

The suspension, not age or skill, decides who can go now: only the exempt categories and the farm quota above <sup>[N3 (2024)](#n3), [N4](#n4)</sup>. Women were 6,636 of those cleared for Oman in 2023, then 56 in 2024, 5 in 2025 and 3 in 2026 to 8 November <sup>[24](#o24), [25](#o25), [26](#o26), [27](#o27)</sup>. Oman's Labour Law lets an employer recruit a foreigner only when no Omani has the qualifications for the job <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - At what age does BMET clear a man for an Oman farm-worker visa in 2026, and is a skill test required?

<a id="jobs-reserved-for-omanis"></a>

#### 1.2.6 Jobs reserved for Omanis

Ministerial Decision 235/2022 bars non-Omanis from the 210 professions in its annex, and Decision 501/2024 added 39 more, numbered 211 to 249 <sup>[28 (2022)](#o28), [N24 (2022)](#n24), [29 (2024)](#o29), [N25 (2024)](#n25)</sup>. They include jobs Bangladeshis used to be sold:
- guard, security guard, warehouse worker and farm tractor driver <sup>[28 (2022)](#o28)</sup>;
- grocer, vegetable and fruit seller and street vendor <sup>[28 (2022)](#o28), [N24 (2022)](#n24)</sup>;
- bus driver and general vehicle driver <sup>[28 (2022)](#o28)</sup>;
- general-maintenance electrical technician, salesman, forklift driver, vehicle and spare-parts seller <sup>[29 (2024)](#o29)</sup>;
- computer programmers from 1 January 2026 <sup>[29 (2024)](#o29)</sup>.

From September 2024 MoL also suspended new work permits for foreign workers, of every nationality, in a number of construction professions (Decision 452/2024) <sup>[23](#o23)</sup>. The Bangladesh Embassy in Muscat said Oman keeps restrictions on foreign hiring under its "Omanisation" policy <sup>[N6](#n6)</sup>.

<a id="what-has-closed-it-before"></a>

#### 1.2.7 What has closed it before

Clearances fell from 188,247 in 2016 to 72,504 in 2018, and to 21,071 in 2020 <sup>[3](#o3)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Did Oman restrict visas for Bangladeshis before 2023 (for example 2017-2019), and on what grounds?

<a id="how-a-job-reaches-you-and-who-takes-a-cut"></a>

### 1.3 How a job reaches you, and who takes a cut

An Oman work visa starts as the Omani employer's MoL authorisation to bring a foreign worker, granted only if no Omani can do the job and the employer meets its Omanisation quota, and the employer pays a work permit fee by occupation class (RO 200, 250 or 300) <sup>[8](#o8), [30](#o30)</sup>. The choke point is that authorisation: whoever holds it, the sponsor (কফিল, kafeel), decides whose name goes on it, and diplomatic sources said Oman shut the corridor in 2023 because such approvals were misused to send workers with no job behind them <sup>[N18 (2023)](#n18)</sup>.

*The free visa (ফ্রি ভিসা, আজাদ ভিসা).* The worker pays a sponsor a fee to work for other employers, and pays the sponsor again at each residence-card renewal <sup>[N8 (2016)](#n8), [N9 (2021)](#n9)</sup>. A Bangladesh labour attaché said an Omani caught doing this is fined 1,000 rials, and the author of an ILO (International Labour Organization) study found the practice widespread <sup>[N9 (2021)](#n9)</sup>. Working for anyone but your sponsor without MoL approval is illegal <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>.

*Down the chain.* A Bangladeshi in Muscat said in 2023 that Bangladeshi recruiters and brokers run the visa trade and employers issue demand letters (the employer's written request for workers) when they need nobody <sup>[N10 (2023)](#n10)</sup>. The secretary-general of BAIRA (the Bangladesh Association of International Recruiting Agencies) said in 2023 that 80% of Oman workers go individually, on visas arranged by family and friends <sup>[N10 (2023)](#n10)</sup>. Long-time migrants often act as recruiters: one woman paid her cousin in Salalah Tk 1.2 lakh for her visa, then recruited about ten maids herself <sup>[N9 (2021)](#n9)</sup>.

*Crackdowns.* In January to June 2026 MoL inspectors found 11,172 foreign workers in violation, including for working without authorisation or outside the approved job; 2,944 were deported and 4,680 cases sent to prosecution <sup>[N26](#n26)</sup>. At Dhaka airport in 2026 immigration found one Oman spouse-visa approval used for five different women <sup>[N14](#n14)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What did an Oman "free visa" sell for in Bangladesh in 2023, before the suspension, and who sells the 2026 farm visas?
> - Has any Omani sponsor or Bangladeshi broker been prosecuted for selling Oman visas since 2023?

<a id="what-you-pay-and-what-you-earn-and-keep"></a>

### 1.4 What you pay, and what you earn and keep

By Omani law no money may be taken from a recruited worker for employing him, and Bangladesh's maximum for a man going to Oman is Tk 1,00,780 including airfare <sup>[8](#o8), [9 (2019)](#o9)</sup>. Workers really paid Tk 1.8 to 3.5 lakh; a woman who paid Tk 2 lakh took a full year to repay it <sup>[N9 (2021)](#n9), [N10 (2023)](#n10)</sup>.

*What people really paid.*
- Tk 3.08 lakh on average in 2015-2018, by the Bangladesh Bureau of Statistics' cost survey <sup>[N27 (2021)](#n27)</sup>.
- Tk 2.3 to 3 lakh for men on company cleaning visas, Tk 1.8 to 2 lakh for women (2020) <sup>[N9 (2021)](#n9)</sup>.
- Tk 2 to 2.5 lakh for a free visa (2020) <sup>[N9 (2021)](#n9)</sup>.
- Tk 3.5 lakh for a shop job that turned out to be masonry, then no work (2023) <sup>[N10 (2023)](#n10)</sup>.

A former BAIRA secretary-general said workers cannot be sent at the government rate <sup>[N27 (2021)](#n27)</sup>.

*What the job pays.* Oman's minimum wage is set by ministerial decision <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>. A Bangladesh-Oman agreement set 90 rials a month for Bangladeshi workers, but it is not rigorously enforced, and licensed recruiting agencies suggest 80 rials for maids without experience <sup>[N9 (2021)](#n9)</sup>. Maids' minimum pay is fixed by nationality: 160 rials for Filipinas, 120 for Indians and Sri Lankans, 90 for Bangladeshis <sup>[N9 (2021)](#n9)</sup>. Men in cleaning companies earn the minimum and depend on overtime; the lowest-paid man an ILO researcher met earned 70 rials and bought his own food <sup>[N9 (2021)](#n9)</sup>. In November 2023 insiders said a worker who could earn over Tk 40,000 a month before the crisis now earned about Tk 25,000 to 35,000 <sup>[N10 (2023)](#n10)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What do the 2026 farm-worker contracts pay, and do they include food and housing?

*Money home.* Remittances from Oman were USD 2.05 billion in FY2025-26 <sup>[N22](#n22)</sup>. In a 2019 Bangladesh Bank survey, migrants in Oman sent Tk 2.0 lakh a year on average, the lowest of the Gulf countries listed <sup>[9 (2019)](#o9)</sup>.

<a id="what-ties-you-to-the-employer"></a>

### 1.5 What ties you to the employer

You can change employer without the old employer's NOC (no-objection certificate) only after the contract ends or is terminated, and the employer can report you as absent <sup>[N28 (2020)](#n28), [31](#o31)</sup>.

- **Entering.** Only an employer holding MoL authorisation can bring you <sup>[N29 (2023)](#n29)</sup>.
- **Working.** You may work only for your sponsor unless MoL authorises otherwise <sup>[N29 (2023)](#n29)</sup>.
- **Quitting.** You can resign online on MoL's website with up to 30 days' notice <sup>[32](#o32)</sup>.
- **Changing employer.** Since 1 January 2021 you need proof the contract has ended and a new employer with a licence to recruit <sup>[N28 (2020)](#n28)</sup>; before that, leaving without an NOC meant a two-year ban <sup>[N28 (2020)](#n28)</sup>. MoL's transfer service asks for the previous sponsor's approval, and the new employer must have a vacant licence <sup>[33](#o33)</sup>.
- **Absconding reports.** After 7 days' absence the employer can file a desertion report, which protects the employer from fines; you can appeal online for free <sup>[31](#o31), [34](#o34)</sup>.
- **Free visa.** The sponsor charges again for each residence-card renewal: one worker paid 610 rials, 550 of it to the sponsor <sup>[N9 (2021)](#n9)</sup>. A social worker told HRW (Human Rights Watch) that some sponsors keep free-visa workers' passports and secretly report them as absconders <sup>[N8 (2016)](#n8)</sup>.

<a id="what-goes-wrong-most-and-what-people-wrongly-believe"></a>

### 1.6 What goes wrong most, and what people wrongly believe

Before the suspension, many men arrived without a confirmed job and stayed jobless or underpaid, the Bangladesh Embassy in Muscat said in 2023; hundreds of jobless Bangladeshis gathered every morning and afternoon at Al Hail market in Muscat hoping for work <sup>[N16 (2023)](#n16), [N10 (2023)](#n10)</sup>.

- **"Oman's visa is open now."** In August 2026 the Bangladesh Embassy in Muscat warned that posts about open visas or "free visas" are false and told people not to pay anyone <sup>[N6](#n6)</sup>.
- **"Go on a visit visa and change it there."** Oman has banned changing a visit visa into a work visa for all nationalities since 31 October 2023 <sup>[N2 (2023)](#n2)</sup>.
- **"The farm quota means everyone can go."** The Bangladesh Embassy in Muscat said the farm-worker notice covered only limited visas for farm work in the Al Dhahirah region <sup>[N6](#n6)</sup>.
- **"A spouse visa is a way in."** In 2026 immigration at Dhaka airport caught one spouse-visa approval used for five different women <sup>[N14](#n14)</sup>.

*How the irregular routes end.* Deportation, as for 2,944 foreign workers of all nationalities in the first half of 2026 <sup>[N26](#n26)</sup>. Some jobless men are trafficked towards Europe through Iran or Turkey <sup>[N16 (2023)](#n16), [N18 (2023)](#n18)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How many Bangladeshi workers' bodies came home from Oman in 2024-2026?

<a id="visas-not-open-to-bangladeshis"></a>

## 2 Visas not open to Bangladeshis

Everything outside the June 2024 exemptions is closed by the October 2023 suspension (see Is it open now) <sup>[N2 (2023)](#n2), [N3 (2024)](#n3)</sup>. On 9 November 2026 ROP's online eligibility check offered a Bangladesh passport holder living outside the GCC only one visa he can get without a sponsor: the 33A Property Owner Visa, for someone who has bought a home in an approved tourist complex <sup>[35](#o35)</sup>.

These schemes exist but are closed to a Bangladesh passport holder, or open only in the narrow cases shown:

- **14-day visa-free entry:** visitors from more than 100 countries and regions may stay up to 14 days without a visa, if they show a passport valid 6 months, a return ticket, a confirmed hotel booking, health insurance and enough money <sup>[36](#o36)</sup>. Bangladesh is on neither ROP list: List 1 (80 countries that need no tourist visa at all) and List 2 (24 nationalities, including India, Bhutan and the Maldives, who qualify only with a valid US, Canada, Australia, UK, Japan or Schengen visa) <sup>[37](#o37), [36](#o36)</sup>. The 14-day stay can't be extended or changed into any residence visa <sup>[36](#o36)</sup>. A Bangladeshi spouse or child of an exempt national can enter with them under the same exemption <sup>[36](#o36)</sup>.
- **Unsponsored Tourist Visit Visa (type 26, 10 days for RO 5 or 30 days for RO 20, applied for online by the visitor himself):** only for nationals of the countries ROP specifies; Indian nationals, for example, get it with a valid Schengen, US, UK, Canada, Australia or Japan visa, and Nepali spouses and children of eligible travellers get it too, but Bangladesh nationals are offered none of its forms <sup>[38](#o38), [39](#o39), [40](#o40), [35](#o35)</sup>. This is the visa Oman's Foreign Ministry calls the usual one for tourists <sup>[36](#o36)</sup>.
- **One-year multiple-entry visit visa (type 36, RO 50, up to one month per stay):** only for nationals of designated countries; not offered to Bangladeshis <sup>[38](#o38), [41](#o41), [35](#o35)</sup>.
- **Oman/Qatar and Dubai/Oman common visas (types 20 and 21):** only for the eligible nationalities that entered Qatar or Dubai first; not offered to Bangladeshis <sup>[38](#o38), [35](#o35)</sup>.
- **Unsponsored Patient Visa (type 35)** for someone with a medical problem: not offered to Bangladeshis (see [Medical treatment visit](#medical-treatment-visit)) <sup>[38](#o38), [35](#o35)</sup>.
- **Truck Driver Visa (12B):** offered online to Pakistani, Indian and Nepali drivers, not to Bangladeshis <sup>[42](#o42), [39](#o39), [40](#o40), [35](#o35)</sup>.
- **Changing a visit or tourist visa into a work visa inside Oman:** stopped for every nationality from 31 October 2023; a visitor who finds a job must leave and come back on a work visa, and for Bangladeshis the work visa itself is suspended (see [Background](#background)) <sup>[N2 (2023)](#n2), [N16 (2023)](#n16)</sup>.
- **Short Work Visa (type 24) and Contractor Visit Visa (type 13):** short visas an Omani employer requests for temporary work or for a pre-employment test; they fall under the October 2023 suspension and are not in the June 2024 exemptions <sup>[43](#o43), [N2 (2023)](#n2), [N3 (2024)](#n3)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Has ROP lifted the suspension for any further category since June 2024, for example the 5,000 agricultural worker visas announced in August 2026? <sup>[N3 (2024)](#n3), [N11](#n11)</sup>
> - Does ROP still treat Bangladesh as a restricted nationality for the sponsored tourist visa (type 6) and the express visa (type 8), or can an Omani sponsor now get them through the e-visa system? <sup>[43](#o43), [35](#o35)</sup>

**Open without any visa:** holders of Bangladeshi diplomatic, official, special and service passports, under an agreement in force since 14 July 2022 <sup>[44](#o44)</sup>.

<a id="work-visa-company-worker"></a>

## 3 Work visa: company worker

Since 31 October 2023 the ROP has issued no new visas to Bangladeshis outside the categories exempted in June 2024, and none of those is a low-skilled job (see Is it open now) <sup>[N2 (2023)](#n2), [N3 (2024)](#n3), [N19 (2024)](#n19)</sup>. The one opening for ordinary workers is the 5,000 farm-worker quota for members of Omani agricultural associations ([Step 1: Check that your route is open](#step-1-check-that-your-route-is-open)), which the Bangladesh Embassy in Muscat said was not a general reopening <sup>[N4](#n4), [N30](#n30)</sup>. BMET cleared 79 workers for Oman from 1 to 8 November 2026, 219 in October and none in April 2026, against about 10,000 Bangladeshis a month before the suspension <sup>[45](#o45), [N19 (2024)](#n19)</sup>.

So the steps below are written for the route as it works when a visa is issued: today that means an exempt profession or the farm-worker quota; for anyone else they describe the route as it worked before 31 October 2023 and will work if the suspension is lifted. The employer gets a labour authorisation from the MoL (Ministry of Labour) and the employment visa from the ROP; in Bangladesh the worker takes the Wafid medical (the Gulf states' pre-departure health check), the employer's papers are attested at the Bangladesh Embassy in Muscat, and BMET gives emigration clearance; in Oman the worker takes a second medical and gets a resident card <sup>[46](#o46), [47](#o47), [48 (2023)](#o48), [49](#o49), [50](#o50), [51](#o51)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Is any ordinary work visa for Bangladeshis (outside the exempt professions and the farm-worker quota) being issued in November 2026?
> - What were the 451 workers BMET cleared for Oman in November 2025, and the 219 in October 2026: farm workers, exempt professions, or something else <sup>[45](#o45)</sup>?
> - Was the 5,000-fisheries-worker quota announced in September 2026 approved, and through which agencies <sup>[N11](#n11)</sup>?

<a id="who-pays-what"></a>

### 3.1 Who pays what

<a id="what-omani-law-says"></a>

#### 3.1.1 What Omani law says

Under the Labour Law (Royal Decree 53/2023), only an MoL-licensed recruiter may recruit non-Omani workers, and it is prohibited to take any amount from the recruited worker for employing him <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>. Breaking this carries 10 days to a month in prison and/or a fine of RO 1,000 to 2,000, and the licence is cancelled or suspended for at least a year <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>. The employer pays the MoL fees for the labour authorisation and work permit: per worker RO 301 (category 1 occupations), RO 251 (category 2) or RO 201 (category 3), cut by 30% if the firm meets its Omanisation quota (the required share of Omani staff) and doubled if it does not ([Step 4: The employer gets the labour authorisation from MoL](#step-4-the-employer-gets-the-labour-authorisation-from-mol)) <sup>[52](#o52), [N31](#n31)</sup>. The ROP visa costs RO 20 <sup>[46](#o46)</sup>, the medical form in Oman RO 30 <sup>[50](#o50)</sup> and the resident card RO 5 a year <sup>[N32](#n32)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Does the Labour Law's ban on taking "any amounts" from workers stop the employer itself, not only a recruiter, from recovering the MoL, ROP and medical fees from the worker <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>?

<a id="the-bangladesh-maximum"></a>

#### 3.1.2 The Bangladesh maximum

The government-set migration cost for Oman is Tk 1,00,780, fixed by the Ministry in 2017, covering the air fare, passport, health fees and related costs <sup>[53 (2019)](#o53), [N33 (2018)](#n33)</sup>. On the posted Oman job orders the employer also pays food, housing, medical cover and the air ticket ([Step 3: Find a real job and check the papers](#step-3-find-a-real-job-and-check-the-papers)) <sup>[48 (2023)](#o48)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Is the 2017 cap of Tk 1,00,780 for Oman still in force, or has a later order replaced it <sup>[53 (2019)](#o53)</sup>?

<a id="what-people-really-pay"></a>

#### 3.1.3 What people really pay

Workers on "free visas" to the Gulf, Oman included, paid about Tk 5 lakh on average against a government-set cost of Tk 1.16 lakh, and 43% found no work on arrival, in a survey by OKUP (Ovibashi Karmi Unnayan Program, a Bangladeshi migrant workers' organisation) <sup>[N34](#n34)</sup>. 72% borrowed, some mortgaging or selling land <sup>[N34](#n34)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - In 2026, what does an Oman farm-worker visa under the 5,000 quota sell for in Bangladesh, and what does the Omani farm owner charge for it?
> - What did an Oman company visa cost a worker in 2023, just before the suspension?

<a id="how-to-pay"></a>

#### 3.1.4 How to pay

Pay the agency only through the bank, into the agency's own account, and keep every receipt <sup>[54](#o54)</sup>. 54% of free-visa migrants paid illegal broker networks and only 7% went through licensed agencies <sup>[N34](#n34)</sup>.

<a id="step-1-check-that-your-route-is-open"></a>

### 3.2 Step 1: Check that your route is open

1. <a id="step-1-check-that-your-route-is-open-item-1"></a>**Exempt professions.** If you are a physician, engineer, nurse, teacher or accountant, the Oman Embassy in Dhaka takes the application, coordinates with the ROP, needs documents "duly attested" to prove you belong to the category, and takes one to four weeks <sup>[N3 (2024)](#n3)</sup>. The Embassy is at Plot No. 1, Road No. 68, Gulshan 2, Dhaka; +880 2-984 0398, +880 2-984 0332, dhaka@fm.gov.om <sup>[44](#o44)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Where and how does a Bangladeshi engineer or nurse lodge an exempt-category work visa at the Oman Embassy in Dhaka, and what attestation does it want?

2. <a id="step-1-check-that-your-route-is-open-item-2"></a>**Farm workers (the 5,000 quota).** Only Omani farm owners can apply, not you <sup>[N4](#n4)</sup>. In August 2026 the Omani Agricultural Association's Al Dhahirah branch took applications from members who had paid its RO 25 annual fee, with their farm-holding (hiyaza) certificate, ID card, a valid MoL work permit and their 2026/27 cultivation form, from 9 to 20 August at its office in Al Murtafa'a, Ibri <sup>[N4](#n4)</sup>. A Sanad office owner (Sanad offices are Oman's licensed government-services counters) told the Oman Observer that each governorate or association can recruit up to 330 workers within the 5,000 <sup>[N4](#n4)</sup>. An individual farm owner's MoL fee is RO 141 a worker for up to 3 workers and RO 241 for 4 or more <sup>[52](#o52)</sup>. The association's number is 99413998 <sup>[N4](#n4)</sup>.
3. <a id="step-1-check-that-your-route-is-open-item-3"></a>**Everyone else.** The Bangladesh Embassy in Muscat warned on 12 August 2026 against social-media claims that "Oman's labour market has opened", "mass visas have started" or "free visas are being given", and asked people not to pay anyone on that basis <sup>[N30](#n30)</sup>. Check any offer with the Embassy, the Ministry of Expatriates' Welfare, BMET or the Omani authorities <sup>[N30](#n30)</sup>.
4. <a id="step-1-check-that-your-route-is-open-item-4"></a>**Age.** You must be at least 21 for an Oman employment visa <sup>[46](#o46), [55](#o55)</sup>.
5. <a id="step-1-check-that-your-route-is-open-item-5"></a>**Omanisation.** An employer may recruit a non-Omani only if no Omani has the skills, it meets its Omanisation plan and quota, and the worker's skills are needed; some occupations are closed to non-Omanis, and MoL can suspend labour authorisations for others <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>. MoL also lists occupations that need a certificate to practise <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Which MoL decision lists the occupations closed to non-Omanis in 2026, and which are suspended for new recruitment <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>?
   > - Has MoL's list of occupations that need a professional certificate been issued, and is there a test centre in Bangladesh <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>?

<a id="step-2-oep-registration-passport-police-clearance-and-certificates"></a>

### 3.3 Step 2: OEP registration, passport, police clearance and certificates

1. <a id="step-2-oep-registration-passport-police-clearance-and-certificates-item-1"></a>**Register on OEP.** OEP (the Overseas Employment Platform, BMET's online system) is where you apply online for training at a TTC (Technical Training Centre), the compulsory pre-departure orientation and emigration clearance <sup>[54](#o54)</sup>.

   You'll need <sup>[56](#o56), [57](#o57)</sup>:
   - your NID (national ID card);
   - a mobile phone that can receive codes;
   - a bKash, Nagad or other mobile-banking account to pay the Tk 200 government fee.

   1. In Google Chrome open employee.oep.gov.bd/employee/login-with-otp (or scan the QR code on BMET's guide) and press "অনুগ্রহ করে এখানে ক্লিক করুন" (Please click here) twice <sup>[56](#o56)</sup>.
   2. Type every detail exactly as on your NID, with no dots in the name and spaces where the NID has them, and press "যাচাই করুন" (Verify) <sup>[56](#o56)</sup>.
   3. Type the OTP (one-time code) sent to your mobile and press Submit, then "সংরক্ষণ করুন ও এগিয়ে যান" (Save and continue), and fill in the full form correctly <sup>[56](#o56)</sup>.
   4. Press "পেমেন্ট করতে এগিয়ে যান" (Proceed to payment), then "পেমেন্ট করুন" (Pay); choose Mobile Banking, then bKash, Nagad or another service, and enter the account number, OTP and PIN <sup>[56](#o56)</sup>.
   5. Download your Registration Card <sup>[56](#o56)</sup>.
   6. Take 2 copies of the registration slip to your DEMO (district employment and manpower office) to give your fingerprints <sup>[57](#o57)</sup>.

   **If it fails:** OEP hotline 09614445271 <sup>[58](#o58)</sup>, or Probash-Bondhu, the 24-hour call centre of the Wage Earners' Welfare Board (WEWB, the Ministry's welfare body for migrant workers): 16135 free in Bangladesh, +8809610102030 from abroad <sup>[59](#o59)</sup>.

2. <a id="step-2-oep-registration-passport-police-clearance-and-certificates-item-2"></a>**Get a passport valid at least 6 months.** The ROP needs a passport copy valid for at least 6 months <sup>[46](#o46)</sup>.

   You'll need: your original NID; applicants over 20 must use the NID <sup>[60](#o60)</sup>.

   1. Fill in the e-passport application online at epassport.gov.bd, exactly as on your NID <sup>[60](#o60)</sup>.
   2. Pay online through A-Challan (the government's online payment-slip system) or the gateways DGePay or ShurjoPay (by card, bKash, Nagad or Rocket), or at any bank through A-Challan <sup>[61](#o61)</sup>. A 48-page e-passport for 5 years costs Tk 4,025 (regular), Tk 6,325 (express) or Tk 8,625 (super express); for 10 years Tk 5,750, Tk 8,050 or Tk 10,350, VAT included <sup>[61](#o61)</sup>.
   3. Bring the original NID to the divisional passport and visa office or regional passport office for your present address <sup>[60](#o60)</sup>. Delivery after biometrics is within 15 working days (regular), 7 (express) or 2 (super express) <sup>[61](#o61)</sup>.

3. <a id="step-2-oep-registration-passport-police-clearance-and-certificates-item-3"></a>**Police clearance certificate (PCC), only if someone asks for it.** ROP's list of documents for a work visa (photos, passport copy, MoL labour permit, the attested medical, and approvals for some occupations) has no police clearance <sup>[46](#o46)</sup>. The Bangladesh Embassy in Muscat treats a Bangladeshi police clearance as valid for 6 months from issue <sup>[62](#o62)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Do Omani employers or Bangladeshi agencies ask Oman workers for a police clearance, and for which jobs?

   You'll need <sup>[63](#o63)</sup>:
   - a passport valid at least 3 months;
   - to live at your present address, which must be the permanent or emergency address in the passport;
   - a scan of the passport data page attested by a first-class gazetted officer;
   - an attested copy of your NID, birth certificate or ward councillor certificate, if your MRP (machine-readable passport) shows no address.

   1. Open pcc.police.gov.bd, register, log in and press Apply; fill in personal details, then addresses, upload the scans, check and submit; nothing can be changed afterwards <sup>[63](#o63)</sup>.
   2. Pay Tk 1,500 by treasury challan, code 1-7301-0001-2681, at any Bangladesh Bank or Sonali Bank branch, or online by card with a service charge <sup>[63](#o63)</sup>.

      > **Need to know**
      > - Is the fee Tk 1,500 (the portal) or Tk 500 (Bangladesh Police's guidance) <sup>[63](#o63), [64](#o64)</sup>?

   3. The police verify you at your present address; track it by texting "PCC S <reference number>" to 26969 <sup>[63](#o63)</sup>.
   4. Collect it with a copy of your passport or application <sup>[63](#o63)</sup>. If you are abroad, someone else can collect it if you upload, with the application, an attested authorisation letter naming them and their NID number <sup>[63](#o63)</sup>. Certificates are issued in English <sup>[64](#o64)</sup>.

   **If it fails:** the PCC helpdesk, Sunday to Thursday 9 am to 5 pm, 01320001824, 01320001825, help.pcc@police.gov.bd <sup>[63](#o63)</sup>; Dhaka Metropolitan Police one-stop service, 01320037339, 01320037329 <sup>[65](#o65)</sup>.

4. <a id="step-2-oep-registration-passport-police-clearance-and-certificates-item-4"></a>**Certificates (educational, experience, driving licence), if your job needs them.** An exempt-profession application needs documents "duly attested" <sup>[N3 (2024)](#n3)</sup>, and a professional body's approval is needed for teaching, religious, media and medical jobs ([Step 6: The employer gets the employment visa from the ROP; check it](#step-6-the-employer-gets-the-employment-visa-from-the-rop-check-it)) <sup>[46](#o46)</sup>.
   1. **In Bangladesh, the attestation chain:** SSC and HSC (Secondary and Higher Secondary School Certificate) go to the Education Board, then the Ministry of Education; degrees to the university, then the Ministry of Education <sup>[66](#o66)</sup>. Experience certificates, a passport copy or NID papers go to a notary, then the Law Ministry; a driving licence to a notary and BRTA (the Bangladesh Road Transport Authority), then the Law Ministry <sup>[66](#o66)</sup>. Then MOFA (Bangladesh's Ministry of Foreign Affairs) attests it <sup>[66](#o66)</sup>. MyGov (mygov.bd, the government's online services portal) lists MOFA's "Certificate attestation for foreign-bound citizens" service, and you check an application's status at mygov.bd (application-latest-status) <sup>[67](#o67), [68](#o68)</sup>.
   2. **Or an apostille.** Bangladesh joined the Apostille Convention (under which member countries accept one apostille certificate instead of embassy legalisation) in force from 30 March 2025, and Oman has been a member since 2012 <sup>[69](#o69)</sup>. Oman is not among the states that objected to Bangladesh joining, and MOFA lists Oman among the 114 countries that accept Bangladeshi apostilles <sup>[70](#o70), [71](#o71)</sup>. MOFA's e-Apostille is applied for on MyGov, takes about five days and carries a QR code, but MOFA says acceptance abroad is still being worked on <sup>[72](#o72)</sup>.
   3. **Oman's attestation.** Since 16 February 2026 Oman's electronic attestation service covers Bangladesh, so documents can be attested without visiting the Oman Embassy in Dhaka; apply at omanpost.om/attestation-services <sup>[73](#o73)</sup>. In Oman, the Foreign Ministry attests only through Oman Post, online or at branches (Muscat Airport Heights or Qurum, Sur, Sohar, Nizwa, Salalah, Buraimi, Khasab, Madha); a study certificate costs RO 10 <sup>[74](#o74)</sup>. The Bangladesh Embassy in Muscat attests Bangladeshi certificates only after MOFA in Dhaka has <sup>[62](#o62)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Do ROP and MoL accept a Bangladeshi e-Apostille on educational certificates for an employment visa, or do they want Oman's e-attestation through Oman Post?
   > - What does Oman's e-attestation through Oman Post charge for a Bangladeshi certificate, and how long does it take <sup>[73](#o73)</sup>?

   **If it fails:** MOFA Bangladesh document authentication, Segunbagicha, Dhaka 1000, +8802-223381852, dgcnw@mofa.gov.bd <sup>[68](#o68)</sup>.

<a id="step-3-find-a-real-job-and-check-the-papers"></a>

### 3.4 Step 3: Find a real job and check the papers

1. <a id="step-3-find-a-real-job-and-check-the-papers-item-1"></a>**What a real Oman job order looks like.** The Omani employer gives a Bangladeshi agency a power of attorney and a demand letter; the Bangladesh Embassy in Muscat's labour wing attests them; the Ministry approves; and BMET issues a permission letter to the agency <sup>[75 (2023)](#o75), [48 (2023)](#o48)</sup>. A real permission letter shows the Embassy's block visa number (for example "BEM/04/010/2023-122"), the visa issue date, "Attested: Yes", the Ministry reference, the employer, the job, the number of workers, the salary, the contract length and whether food, housing, medical cover, the air ticket and overtime are provided <sup>[48 (2023)](#o48)</sup>.
2. <a id="step-3-find-a-real-job-and-check-the-papers-item-2"></a>**Which agencies held Oman job orders.** BMET posted four Oman permission letters, all in August to October 2023, just before the suspension: 370 posts in all <sup>[48 (2023)](#o48), [76 (2023)](#o76), [77 (2023)](#o77), [75 (2023)](#o75)</sup>:
   - **ProSearch Recruitment Consultants (RL1027):** 150 skilled helpers (22 Aug 2023) and 100 helpers (19 Oct 2023) for Galfar Engineering & Contracting SAOG, Muscat, at RO 100 a month for 2 years, with food, housing, medical cover and air ticket, no overtime <sup>[48 (2023)](#o48), [76 (2023)](#o76)</sup>.
   - **Ahmed & Company (RL0081):** 30 dish washers for Al Naba Supplies & Catering Services LLC, Ruwi, at RO 90 a month basic with free food, the ticket home only after two years (Sep 2023); and 90 welders, steel fitters, scaffolders and blaster/sprayers for Asyad Dry Dock Company LLC at RO 186 (19 Oct 2023) <sup>[75 (2023)](#o75), [77 (2023)](#o77)</sup>.

   A search of BMET's list (bmet.gov.bd/pages/miscellaneous-infos) for ওমান or Oman returns nothing, so the search box does not find these letters <sup>[78](#o78), [79](#o79)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Have any Oman permission letters been issued since BMET stopped posting letters in December 2025, for example for the farm-worker quota?
   > - What maximum fee did BMET allow the agency to take on the four 2023 Oman letters (the Bangla pages are scans)?

3. <a id="step-3-find-a-real-job-and-check-the-papers-item-3"></a>**Check the demand letter at the Embassy.** The Embassy's labour wing in Muscat attests demand letters; contact the Counsellor (Labour), Mohd. Rafiul Islam, +968 24698440, clomanbd@mail.com, or the First Secretary (Labour), Asadul Haque, +968 24698440, ssmuscat@probashi.gov.bd <sup>[75 (2023)](#o75), [80](#o80)</sup>. For visa or agreement attestation, as for every Embassy service, the applicant first pays the fee at Bank Muscat, then fills in the Embassy's online form (forms.gle/CYHtStaaKWC1afwN6), then attends on the Embassy's published schedule <sup>[81](#o81)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - What papers does the Muscat labour wing need to attest a demand letter and power of attorney, what does it charge, and how long does it take?
   > - Can a worker in Bangladesh ask the labour wing to confirm a "BEM/04/010/..." block visa number <sup>[48 (2023)](#o48)</sup>?

4. <a id="step-3-find-a-real-job-and-check-the-papers-item-4"></a>**Have BMET check the visa.** BMET checks job visas and demand letters free in 3 working days, but its online check covers Saudi Arabia, Qatar, Bahrain, Singapore and the UAE, not Oman <sup>[49](#o49)</sup>. Bring the visa and the passport page with the visa stamp to BMET in Dhaka or your DEMO <sup>[49](#o49), [54](#o54)</sup>.
5. <a id="step-3-find-a-real-job-and-check-the-papers-item-5"></a>**If a relative or friend in Oman sends you a visa.** Only an employer resident in Oman can sponsor, and the visa is for one employer and one occupation, which must match the labour permit <sup>[82](#o82), [46](#o46)</sup>. Working for anyone other than the employer on your permit gets your permit cancelled, deportation and a ban on entering Oman <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>. A worker who got his own visa applies for BMET clearance himself ([Step 7: Orientation, BMET clearance, insurance, a loan and the flight](#step-7-orientation-bmet-clearance-insurance-a-loan-and-the-flight)) <sup>[49](#o49)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Before the suspension, what did a relative in Oman charge for a single company visa, and is a single visa attested by the Muscat Embassy before BMET clears it?

<a id="step-4-the-employer-gets-the-labour-authorisation-from-mol"></a>

### 3.5 Step 4: The employer gets the labour authorisation from MoL

1. <a id="step-4-the-employer-gets-the-labour-authorisation-from-mol-item-1"></a>**The employer applies to MoL** for a labour authorisation: MoL's written approval to bring a non-Omani worker for a named occupation, valid 24 months and not renewable <sup>[52](#o52)</sup>. MoL grants it only under the Omanisation conditions in [Step 1: Check that your route is open](#step-1-check-that-your-route-is-open) <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>.
2. <a id="step-4-the-employer-gets-the-labour-authorisation-from-mol-item-2"></a>**The employer pays after approval:** RO 301, 251 or 201 per worker by occupation category, under Ministerial Decision 602/2025, in force since 15 February 2026 <sup>[52](#o52), [N31](#n31), [83](#o83)</sup>. The fee is refundable, or a new licence costs RO 1, if you fail the medical, if the ROP refuses the visa, or if the employer sends you home within 90 days <sup>[52](#o52), [N31](#n31)</sup>.
3. <a id="step-4-the-employer-gets-the-labour-authorisation-from-mol-item-3"></a>**The contract.** It must be written in Arabic (or with an approved Arabic copy attached), in two copies, one for you, and approved by MoL; registering it costs the employer RO 1 <sup>[8](#o8), [N31](#n31)</sup>. A fixed-term contract may not exceed 5 years <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>. If there is no written contract you can prove your rights by any means <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - How long does an MoL labour authorisation take in 2026, and can a worker abroad check it online?

<a id="step-5-the-medical-in-bangladesh-wafid"></a>

### 3.6 Step 5: The medical in Bangladesh (Wafid)

1. <a id="step-5-the-medical-in-bangladesh-wafid-item-1"></a>**Book the slip.** The agency or you book on wafid.com (the Gulf Health Council's booking system, formerly GAMCA): tap "Book an Appointment", choose the country, the city and Oman as the travelling country, and pay by card; the centre is assigned by city <sup>[84](#o84)</sup>. A Standard booking costs USD 10; a Premium booking at USD 25 lets you choose the centre and date <sup>[85](#o85)</sup>. The booking, called the "slip", can be reprinted from "Print GCC slip" with your passport number and nationality <sup>[84](#o84)</sup>.
2. <a id="step-5-the-medical-in-bangladesh-wafid-item-2"></a>**Pay the centre.** The whole medical should cost no more than Tk 8,500 to 9,000 <sup>[N35](#n35)</sup>; one worker paid Tk 8,500 at a Chattogram centre <sup>[N36](#n36)</sup>.
3. <a id="step-5-the-medical-in-bangladesh-wafid-item-3"></a>**The result** appears within 26 days and is valid for 2 months for the visa <sup>[84](#o84), [86 (2021)](#o86)</sup>. Unfit conditions include HIV, hepatitis B and C, malaria, leprosy, abnormal chest X-rays including past TB, uncontrolled blood pressure or diabetes, and cancer <sup>[84](#o84)</sup>. If a valid report is already in the system, you can rebook only 2 months after it <sup>[84](#o84)</sup>. Complaints go to the Gulf Health Council within two weeks <sup>[86 (2021)](#o86)</sup>.
4. <a id="step-5-the-medical-in-bangladesh-wafid-item-4"></a>**The employer attests it in Oman.** The employer attests your Wafid e-report on MoH's (Ministry of Health's) eHealth Portal for RO 2, which takes 5 minutes, and then completes the visa on the ROP site <sup>[47](#o47)</sup>.

What happens in practice: a former official of BAIRA (the Bangladesh Association of International Recruiting Agencies) said only 2 to 5% of workers get the medical at the set fee and the rest pay at least Tk 17,000 to 20,000 <sup>[N36](#n36)</sup>. Centres allegedly declare people unfit first and then demand a bribe for a "fit" report <sup>[N36](#n36)</sup>; see [Scams](#scams).

<a id="step-6-the-employer-gets-the-employment-visa-from-the-rop-check-it"></a>

### 3.7 Step 6: The employer gets the employment visa from the ROP; check it

1. <a id="step-6-the-employer-gets-the-employment-visa-from-the-rop-check-it-item-1"></a>**The employer's PRO** (public relations officer, the company's government clerk) applies on the ROP e-visa system after registering the company with the Directorate General of Passports and Residence <sup>[87](#o87)</sup>. The visa needs two 6x4 cm photos, your passport copy, the MoL labour permit and your medical certificate attested by MoH, plus a professional body's approval for teaching, religious, media and medical jobs <sup>[46](#o46)</sup>. If you worked in Oman and left less than two years ago, it also needs a release letter from your previous employer approved by the Directorate General of Passports and Residence <sup>[46](#o46)</sup>. It costs RO 20 <sup>[46](#o46)</sup>.
2. <a id="step-6-the-employer-gets-the-employment-visa-from-the-rop-check-it-item-2"></a>**What you get.** An employment visa for a worker aged 21 or over holding an MoL work permit, valid for 2 years from stamping and multi-entry <sup>[55](#o55), [46](#o46)</sup>. Your gender and occupation must match the labour permit <sup>[46](#o46)</sup>.
3. <a id="step-6-the-employer-gets-the-employment-visa-from-the-rop-check-it-item-3"></a>**Check it.** Only the sponsor can see the status, by logging in to its eVisa account ("Find Visa Application"), and it can reprint the approved visa as a PDF by passport number <sup>[88](#o88)</sup>. Ask the employer for that PDF, and call the ROP eVisa centre on +968 22848200 from Bangladesh (80080011 in Oman) or email Info-OmaneVisa@rop.gov.om <sup>[88](#o88)</sup>.

4. <a id="step-6-the-employer-gets-the-employment-visa-from-the-rop-check-it-item-4"></a>**If it is refused.** ROP can reject any application without giving a reason <sup>[89](#o89)</sup>. The employer then gets back its MoL fee, or a new labour authorisation for RO 1 ([Step 4: The employer gets the labour authorisation from MoL](#step-4-the-employer-gets-the-labour-authorisation-from-mol)) <sup>[52](#o52), [N31](#n31)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Is there any ROP page where a worker can check an Oman employment visa with his own passport number?
   > - Can an employer appeal a refused employment visa, and how long must it wait before applying again for the same worker?

<a id="step-7-orientation-bmet-clearance-insurance-a-loan-and-the-flight"></a>

### 3.8 Step 7: Orientation, BMET clearance, insurance, a loan and the flight

1. <a id="step-7-orientation-bmet-clearance-insurance-a-loan-and-the-flight-item-1"></a>**Do the compulsory 3-day PDO** (pre-departure orientation) at one of 70 training institutes: apply on OEP and pay the institute's fee against a money receipt <sup>[49](#o49), [54](#o54)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - What is the click path for booking the PDO on OEP, and do workers going back to Oman have to repeat it?

2. <a id="step-7-orientation-bmet-clearance-insurance-a-loan-and-the-flight-item-2"></a>**Get BMET clearance (the smart card).** The smart card is your emigration clearance; with it you need no separate Welfare Board membership <sup>[90](#o90)</sup>.
   1. **If an agency recruited you,** it applies after the visa with its application, the visa copy, the attested contract (where applicable), an attested copy of your registration card, its undertaking on job certainty on Tk 300 non-judicial stamp paper, the original bank pay order for the welfare and smart card fees, and the original source income-tax challan; BMET's emigration branch clears within 3 working days <sup>[49](#o49)</sup>.
   2. **If you got the visa yourself,** apply at BMET's emigration branch in Dhaka or the Chattogram DEMO with your application, the visa copy, the contract if any, an attested copy of your registration card, a declaration on Tk 300 stamp paper and pay orders for the welfare and insurance fees; 3 working days <sup>[49](#o49)</sup>.
   3. **Fees:**
      - welfare fee: Tk 5,500 from 1 October 2026 <sup>[91](#o91)</sup>;
      - insurance premium: Tk 1,000, once <sup>[54](#o54)</sup>;
      - clearance fee: Tk 250 per worker <sup>[49](#o49)</sup>;
      - source income tax: Tk 400 (single visa, not attested) or Tk 250 (attested), and Tk 1,200 (skilled) or Tk 800 (unskilled) on a group visa <sup>[49](#o49)</sup>;
      - smart card fee: Tk 250 <sup>[49](#o49)</sup>.

   **Check it is real** at portal.wewb.gov.bd/bmet-wewbm-check <sup>[90](#o90)</sup>. Welfare desks at Dhaka, Chattogram and Sylhet airports check smart cards before departure <sup>[49](#o49)</sup>; in 2023-24 they stopped 1,098 travellers with no or fake clearance <sup>[92 (2024)](#o92)</sup>. **If it goes wrong:** BMET Director (Emigration), +8802222222415, 01711-135901, diremi@bmet.gov.bd; then the Additional Director General (Employment), 02-48321949, adgemp@bmet.gov.bd <sup>[49](#o49)</sup>.

3. <a id="step-7-orientation-bmet-clearance-insurance-a-loan-and-the-flight-item-3"></a>**Know your insurance.** Jiban Bima Corporation (JBC, the state insurer) insures workers who leave with BMET clearance on or after 12 March 2023, for Tk 10 lakh, for 5 years, for workers aged 18 to 55, for the one-time Tk 1,000 <sup>[54](#o54), [93 (2024)](#o93)</sup>. It pays Tk 50,000 if you lose the job and return within 6 months of going, except to workers who went entirely at the employer's cost <sup>[93 (2024)](#o93)</sup>.
4. <a id="step-7-orientation-bmet-clearance-insurance-a-loan-and-the-flight-item-4"></a>**If you need a loan,** PKB (Probashi Kallyan Bank, the government bank for migrant workers) lends up to Tk 3 lakh at 9% simple interest a year, repaid in equal monthly instalments over up to 3 years including a grace period; the service time is 7 working days <sup>[94](#o94), [95 (2019)](#o95)</sup>. Women migrants have a separate loan on the same terms <sup>[96](#o96)</sup>. You must be 18 to 60 and not in default on any loan, and you and your guarantors must live permanently in the branch's area <sup>[94](#o94)</sup>. A worker going back on a re-entry visa also gives proof of remitting through legal channels, proof of employment abroad and a statement of migration costs <sup>[94](#o94)</sup>.

   You'll need <sup>[94](#o94)</sup>:
   - the free application form;
   - 3 recent photos;
   - copies of your NID, passport, visa and BMET card, and the contract if you have one;
   - 3 signed MICR (magnetic-ink, machine-readable) cheque leaves from your own active bank account, with its statement;
   - a PKB savings account opened with at least Tk 500;
   - two guarantors: one financially solvent person and one family member <sup>[94](#o94)</sup>.

   1. Apply at the PKB branch for your permanent address, or online on mygov.bd, uploading your photo, passport, visa, BMET card and NID and your guarantors' NIDs and photos; you can apply only once until the loan is approved or refused, and you pay nothing when you submit <sup>[94](#o94), [95 (2019)](#o95)</sup>.
   2. After the decision, pay processing and documentation fees of 0.5% each plus VAT, Tk 900 per lakh for risk cover, and 16 revenue stamps of Tk 10 plus 3 adhesive stamps of Tk 100 per guarantor <sup>[94](#o94)</sup>.

   **If it goes wrong:** PKB helpline 16238, +880 9677-787878 from abroad <sup>[94](#o94)</sup>; loans department 01716-175560, migration@pkb.gov.bd <sup>[97](#o97)</sup>.

5. <a id="step-7-orientation-bmet-clearance-insurance-a-loan-and-the-flight-item-5"></a>**The flight.** Before you go, check you have <sup>[54](#o54)</sup>:
   - a valid passport;
   - the employment visa;
   - the medical certificate;
   - the demand letter;
   - the contract, attested by the embassy;
   - the agency's name, address and phone;
   - the BMET clearance or smart card;
   - the air ticket.

   Keep one set of copies of all papers at home and at least 3 with you, and open at least 2 bank accounts before going, one in your name and one in a family member's <sup>[54](#o54)</sup>. Never carry a packet whose contents you don't know <sup>[54](#o54)</sup>.

<a id="step-8-after-you-arrive"></a>

### 3.9 Step 8: After you arrive

1. <a id="step-8-after-you-arrive-item-1"></a>**The medical in Oman.** The employer gets your medical form on MoH's eHealth Portal or at a Sanad office for RO 30, or RO 40 for a food handler; it is valid for 30 days <sup>[50](#o50)</sup>. You show the QR code it generates at the nearest private medical centre that does labour screening <sup>[50](#o50)</sup>. The Ministry of Health re-examines every arriving worker, and a centre in Bangladesh that passed an unfit worker can be penalised <sup>[86 (2021)](#o86)</sup>. If you are found unfit on first arrival, MoL rules govern your return home and the employer can recover its fee <sup>[8](#o8), [52](#o52)</sup>.
2. <a id="step-8-after-you-arrive-item-2"></a>**The resident card within 30 days.** Every resident must get a resident card from the ROP within 30 days of entry, in person, with the application form, the original passport and the medical form <sup>[51](#o51)</sup>. It costs RO 5 for each year of validity, up to 10 years, and RO 20 to replace; renew it within 30 days of expiry <sup>[N32](#n32)</sup>. The ROP page lists a fine of 10 for each month of delay <sup>[51](#o51)</sup>. MoL's guidance calls this card your "labour card" <sup>[98](#o98)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Does ROP charge RO 5 a year for a resident card (Decision 157/2025), or still RO 6 for one year and RO 11 for two, as its resident-card page says <sup>[N32](#n32), [51](#o51)</sup>?

3. <a id="step-8-after-you-arrive-item-3"></a>**Worker registration.** The employer must register your data with MoL; it is fined RO 10 a month from your arrival if late, up to RO 500 <sup>[52](#o52), [N31](#n31)</sup>.
4. <a id="step-8-after-you-arrive-item-4"></a>**Check it was done.** MoL's free "Check Your Employment Record in the Private Sector" service on gov.om shows which companies you are registered with <sup>[99](#o99)</sup>. If your labour or resident card is not done within a month, tell your direct boss, and call MoL on 80077000 <sup>[98](#o98)</sup>. You are entitled to a copy of your contract <sup>[98](#o98)</sup>. Your employer may keep your passport only with your written consent <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Does the employment-record service open with a resident-card login only, or can a newly arrived worker use it?

5. <a id="step-8-after-you-arrive-item-5"></a>**Approve your contract yourself.** MoL has told workers to approve their employment contracts online themselves and to keep hold of their civil ID (resident) card, not hand it to the employer or anyone else to approve the contract for them; the warning is aimed at fictitious employment <sup>[N37](#n37)</sup>.
6. <a id="step-8-after-you-arrive-item-6"></a>**Bank account.** Your wage must be paid into an account in your name (see [Sending money home](#sending-money-home)) <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>.
7. <a id="step-8-after-you-arrive-item-7"></a>**SIM card.** Keep every mobile number registered in your name under your control: in 2026 a man whose registered number was linked to a matter under investigation spent several days in custody, though he said he had nothing to do with it <sup>[N38](#n38)</sup>. Check which connections are registered under your identity and cancel any you do not use <sup>[N38](#n38)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - What does an Omani operator (Omantel, Ooredoo, Vodafone) ask a new worker for to issue a SIM (passport or resident card, fingerprint), how many SIMs may one expatriate hold, and how does he check the numbers registered to his civil number?
   > - Where on the MoL portal does a worker approve his contract, and with which login?

<a id="where-to-get-help-by-stage"></a>

#### 3.9.1 Where to get help, by stage

- **In Bangladesh, before you pay:** BMET's workers' complaint cell, by application with proof to the Director General, free, 30 working days <sup>[49](#o49)</sup>; Probash-Bondhu 16135 <sup>[59](#o59)</sup>.
- **Before an exempt-profession visa:** the Oman Embassy in Dhaka, +880 2-984 0398, dhaka@fm.gov.om <sup>[44](#o44)</sup>.
- **About an Oman visa offer:** the Embassy in Muscat's labour wing ([Step 3: Find a real job and check the papers](#step-3-find-a-real-job-and-check-the-papers)) <sup>[80](#o80)</sup>; the Embassy said it posts any reopening on its Facebook page and website <sup>[N30](#n30)</sup>.
- **At BMET clearance:** BMET Director (Emigration) ([Step 7: Orientation, BMET clearance, insurance, a loan and the flight](#step-7-orientation-bmet-clearance-insurance-a-loan-and-the-flight)) <sup>[49](#o49)</sup>.
- **In Oman:** MoL 80077000 <sup>[98](#o98)</sup>; MoH customer care +968 24441999 for the medical <sup>[50](#o50)</sup>; the Embassy's line for workers' problems, +968 24603514, 8:30 am to 4:30 pm <sup>[100](#o100)</sup>; the Embassy's Expatriate Service Cell, WhatsApp +968 91980621, consularmuscat@gmail.com, and its 24-hour hotline +968 91997852 for extreme emergencies only, such as an accident that has just happened <sup>[101](#o101), [100](#o100)</sup>.

<a id="scams"></a>

### 3.10 Scams

<a id="done-to-workers"></a>

#### 3.10.1 Done to workers

**"Oman is open" and "free visa" offers:** Social-media posts used the August 2026 farm-worker notice to claim that Oman had opened and was giving free visas; the Embassy said no such announcement had been made <sup>[N30](#n30)</sup>. A "free visa" is not a real visa type: Omani visas are issued for one job with one employer, and working for anyone else ends in deportation and an entry ban <sup>[N39 (2018)](#n39), [8](#o8)</sup>. In 2018 Oman detained more than 20,000 Bangladeshis for labour-law breaches with no valid papers <sup>[N39 (2018)](#n39)</sup>. **Spot it:** anyone selling an ordinary Oman work visa in 2026 outside an exempt profession or the farm quota ([Step 1: Check that your route is open](#step-1-check-that-your-route-is-open)) <sup>[N3 (2024)](#n3), [N4](#n4)</sup>.

**Visit visa sold as a job:** Since 31 October 2023 a visit or tourist visa cannot be converted into a work visa inside Oman; you must leave and come back on a work visa <sup>[N2 (2023)](#n2)</sup>. **Spot it:** the paper says tourist, visit or express visa, not employment visa ([Step 6: The employer gets the employment visa from the ROP; check it](#step-6-the-employer-gets-the-employment-visa-from-the-rop-check-it)) <sup>[55](#o55)</sup>.

**Fake or unchecked demand letters:** A real Oman demand letter carries the Embassy's attestation and the employer's power of attorney to the agency, and the permission letter shows the Embassy's block visa number <sup>[75 (2023)](#o75), [48 (2023)](#o48)</sup>. **Spot it:** check with the labour wing ([Step 3: Find a real job and check the papers](#step-3-find-a-real-job-and-check-the-papers)) and have BMET check the papers <sup>[80](#o80), [49](#o49)</sup>.

**Fake or missing BMET clearance:** Airport welfare desks stopped 1,098 travellers with no or fake BMET clearance in 2023-24 <sup>[92 (2024)](#o92)</sup>. **Spot it:** check your smart card at portal.wewb.gov.bd/bmet-wewbm-check before you fly ([Step 7: Orientation, BMET clearance, insurance, a loan and the flight](#step-7-orientation-bmet-clearance-insurance-a-loan-and-the-flight)) <sup>[90](#o90)</sup>.

**Medical-slip rackets:** An agency offered one worker a Tk 10,000 "standard" medical that could end in "unfit", or Tk 20,000 for a guaranteed "fit" <sup>[N35](#n35)</sup>. Syndicate-linked centres pre-book slots, take nearly 90% of referrals and charge Tk 25,000 to 30,000 for a guaranteed "fit" certificate <sup>[N35](#n35)</sup>. A worker who paid Tk 50,000 in Dhaka was certified fit after failing in Chattogram <sup>[N36](#n36)</sup>. Workers found unfit pay Tk 30,000 to 80,000 in bribes for a "fit" certificate, and because one "unfit" result can blacklist a worker for life, many contract a broker before the test to be passed <sup>[N36](#n36)</sup>. A "fit" bought this way fails on arrival: one worker who paid Tk 25,000 was found unfit in Saudi Arabia and deported <sup>[N35](#n35), [86 (2021)](#o86)</sup>. **Spot it:** book only on wafid.com ([Step 5: The medical in Bangladesh (Wafid)](#step-5-the-medical-in-bangladesh-wafid)) <sup>[84](#o84)</sup>.

<a id="tried-by-workers"></a>

#### 3.10.2 Tried by workers

Buying a "fit" certificate does not hold: the Ministry of Health tests you again on arrival <sup>[86 (2021)](#o86)</sup>. Forged certificates or experience are grounds for MoL to send you home <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>. Working for someone other than your sponsor gets your permit cancelled and you deported and banned <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>.

<a id="your-rights-at-work"></a>

### 3.11 Your rights at work

<a id="contract"></a>

#### 3.11.1 Contract

**Which law covers you:** Oman's Labour Law (Royal Decree 53/2023) covers all employers and workers except those whose work is governed by special laws or systems <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>. Domestic workers come under a separate regulation, Ministerial Decision 574/2025: see Work visa: domestic worker <sup>[N40](#n40)</sup>. Royal Decree 66/2026 (June 2026) repealed the Labour Law's Article 146 on workers taking bribes and moved that crime into the Penal Law <sup>[N41](#n41)</sup>. Any contract term, release or waiver that gives you less than the law is void, unless it is better for you <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>. The employer may not impose forced or compulsory labour <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>.

**Your contract:** It must be in writing, in Arabic, in two copies, one for you and one for the employer; it may be in another language too, but an Arabic copy agreed by both sides must be attached, and the contract must be approved by the labour authority <sup>[102](#o102)</sup>. If there is no written contract you can prove your rights by any means <sup>[102](#o102)</sup>. It must state the employer, the establishment and the workplace address, your details, the type of work and its conditions, the period, the basic wage, allowances and benefits, the date the wage is paid, and the notice for not renewing it, which from the employer may not be less than a month <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>. A fixed-term contract lasts at most 5 years and can be renewed by agreement; renewals count towards your total service <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>. A contract becomes open-ended if it is not written, or if a fixed-term contract ends and both sides carry on without a new written agreement <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>. Employer rules and circulars must be in Arabic, and if another language is used too, only the Arabic counts <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>. Since December 2024 the employer must update the registered contract whenever your wage changes, so it shows your actual wage <sup>[103 (2024)](#o103)</sup>. MOL's (the Ministry of Labour's) leaflet for expatriates says you are entitled to a copy of your contract <sup>[98](#o98)</sup>.

The employer may not breach the contract or give you work that was not agreed, except temporarily to prevent or deal with an accident or in force majeure, or similar work that does not harm your rights <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>. Moving you to another worksite or another of its establishments needs your agreement and MOL's authorisation, at the employer's cost <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Can a worker download his registered contract from MOL's portal, as in Qatar, and how?

**Probation:** At most 3 months if you are paid monthly, 2 months otherwise, and only once with the same employer <sup>[102](#o102)</sup>. If you pass, it counts as service <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>. Either side may end the contract during probation if it is not working out, with at least 7 days' notice <sup>[102](#o102)</sup>.

<a id="pay-and-deductions"></a>

#### 3.11.2 Pay and deductions

**Minimum wage:** Oman's minimum wage applies to citizens, not to foreign workers, so your pay is the basic wage and allowances written in your contract <sup>[N42 (2024)](#n42), [8](#o8)</sup>.

**When and how you are paid:** Wages are paid in Omani rials unless another legal currency is agreed <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>. A monthly-paid worker must be paid at least once a month, on a working day, and in all cases within 3 days of the end of the pay period <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>. The employer is not cleared of the wage unless it is transferred to your account at a bank or financial institution licensed by the Central Bank of Oman, and it is the employer that must prove it paid <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>. Paying late breaks the law and carries a fine of RO 100 to 300 for each worker, doubled for a repeat; not paying into your bank account carries RO 500 to 1,000 for each worker, doubled for a repeat <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>.

The WPS (Wage Protection System, MOL's electronic check on salary transfers, under Ministerial Decision 729/2024) requires the employer to transfer wages through it within 3 days of the end of the wage period <sup>[103 (2024)](#o103)</sup>. MOL required at least 75% of each company's workers to be paid through WPS from September 2025 wages and 90% from November 2025 wages <sup>[N43](#n43)</sup>. An employer that breaks the WPS rules can be warned, have new initial work permits stopped until it complies, and be fined RO 50 per worker, doubled for a repeat <sup>[103 (2024)](#o103)</sup>. The employer is excused from WPS for a worker in a labour dispute who has stopped work for over 30 days, a worker suspended for over 30 days for reasons not the employer's, a worker with a work-abandonment report approved more than 30 days ago, a new worker in his first 30 days and a worker on unpaid leave <sup>[103 (2024)](#o103)</sup>. Observers reported in 2025 that only 25% of private businesses were paying through WPS <sup>[N44](#n44)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Is 100% WPS coverage now required, and from which month's wages?
> - Can a worker see his own WPS salary record in an MOL or bank app?

**Deductions:** Wages may be withheld only up to one quarter, for court-ordered maintenance or sums you owe the government or the employer <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>. Loan repayments may not exceed what was agreed, and the employer may not charge interest on loans or advances <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>. For damage you cause deliberately, the employer may, after an investigation and telling you, deduct the cost at up to 25% of your monthly wage; you can complain to MOL within 30 days <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>. A disciplinary fine may not exceed 5 days' wage a month for each violation, and no penalty may be imposed without telling you in writing and hearing your defence <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>. You cannot be forced to buy food or goods from the employer or particular shops <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>, and nothing may be charged or deducted for safety equipment and protection <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>. You cannot be moved from monthly pay to daily, weekly, piece or hourly pay without your written consent <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>. Pay may not be cut for time off to answer a written summons to a court, the Public Prosecution or another authority <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>. Charging a worker anything for recruiting him is banned (see [Who pays what](#who-pays-what)) <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>.

<a id="hours-rest-and-leave"></a>

#### 3.11.3 Hours, rest and leave

**Hours and overtime:** At most 8 actual hours a day and 40 a week, plus a daily hour for rest and food that does not count, and no more than 6 hours in a row; in Ramadan, Muslim workers work at most 6 hours a day or 30 a week <sup>[102](#o102)</sup>. The employer must post the schedule of hours and rest days where you can see it <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>. Overtime may bring the total to at most 12 hours a day and needs your consent; it is paid at your basic wage plus at least 25% for day hours and 50% for night hours (9 pm to 5 am), or with time off <sup>[102](#o102), [8](#o8)</sup>. Work on your rest day or an official holiday earns 100% of your daily basic wage on top of the day's wage, or a day off for each day worked <sup>[102](#o102)</sup>. Overtime without your consent is allowed only for stock-taking and accounts work (at most 15 days a year unless MOL allows more) or emergencies, and is then paid at basic wage plus 50% (day) or 75% (night), or 200% on rest days and holidays <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>. The Minister may exempt seasonal work and specified sectors from these limits <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>. In practice some migrant workers have been reported working more than 12 hours a day without a day off <sup>[N42 (2024)](#n42)</sup>.

**Rest day and holidays:** At least 2 consecutive paid rest days a week, which cannot be taken away because you were absent <sup>[102](#o102)</sup>. You are paid your gross wage on official public holidays <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>.

**Annual leave:** At least 30 days a year on your gross wage, taken after at least 6 months' service; you can carry over up to 30 unused days <sup>[102](#o102)</sup>. The employer may postpone your leave by up to 6 months for work reasons, but you must take at least 30 days once every 2 years <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>. You may not give up your leave, and any balance left when the job ends is paid at your gross wage <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>. You are entitled to a return ticket home for the leave agreed in your contract (see [Going home on leave](#going-home-on-leave)) <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>. An employer that denies rest days, annual leave, holiday pay, sick leave or special leave can be fined RO 100 to 300 for each worker <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>.

**Sick leave:** Up to 182 days a year: days 1 to 21 at 100% of your gross wage, days 22 to 35 at 75%, days 36 to 70 at 50%, days 71 to 182 at 35% <sup>[102](#o102)</sup>. From 19 July 2026 sick and other leave is insured for expatriates by the SPF (Social Protection Fund, Oman's social insurance agency): the employer pays the first 7 days in full, keeps paying the allowance and claims it back from the Fund <sup>[N45](#n45), [104](#o104)</sup>. If illness keeps you off work for at least 3 months in a year after sick and annual leave are used up, the contract ends <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>. In practice employers have cancelled the contracts of seriously sick or injured foreign workers <sup>[N42 (2024)](#n42)</sup>.

**Other paid leave:** 7 days' paternity leave, 3 days for your marriage, 3 days for the death of a parent, grandparent, brother or sister, 2 days for an uncle or aunt, 10 days for your wife or a child, 15 days for Hajj once after a year's service, and 98 days' maternity leave; you must show proof <sup>[102](#o102)</sup>. Maternity leave has been insured by the SPF for expatriate company and government workers since 19 July 2024, but not for domestic workers <sup>[N46](#n46)</sup>.

**Heat: the midday ban:** From 1 June to 31 August, work at construction sites and other open workplaces in the sun must stop between 12:30 pm and 3:30 pm <sup>[105](#o105), [N47](#n47)</sup>. An employer that needs to work then must get an MOL permit and provide enough cold drinking water, an enclosed, furnished, air-conditioned rest area near the site, cool washing water and an air-conditioned bus <sup>[105](#o105)</sup>. In June 2026 MOL sent inspection teams nationwide, and officials said violators face fines of RO 500 to 1,000 plus administrative penalties <sup>[N48](#n48)</sup>. MOL acts on safety reports from workers and the public, and its "Safe Summer" heatstroke guidance is given in Bengali among other languages <sup>[N49](#n49)</sup>. Where there is imminent danger, MOL must close the workplace or stop the equipment until the danger ends <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What is the number or app for a worker to report a midday-ban breach?

<a id="housing-food-and-health-care"></a>

#### 3.11.4 Housing, food and health care

**Housing and food:** The Labour Law requires transport, suitable housing, meals and drinking water only from employers working in areas the Minister specifies <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>. MOL's older leaflet for expatriates says the employer provides suitable food, accommodation and free medical treatment throughout the contract <sup>[98](#o98)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Which areas has the Minister specified under Article 58, and are there current housing standards for labour camps?

**Health care and insurance:** The employer must provide first aid, and a qualified nurse or a contracted health provider where more than 200 workers are in one place <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>. If you are treated in a government or private hospital and have no health insurance, the employer pays for treatment, medicine and the hospital stay <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>. Dhamani, the national health insurance platform run by the FSA (Financial Services Authority), started its pilot phase on 20 April 2025 <sup>[106](#o106)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
